GAME DEVELOPMENT ALGORITHMS

🌐 PATHFINDING & GRAPH SEARCH
NPC navigation through complex game worlds, mazes, and grids
A* Search Algorithm
The gold standard for game pathfinding. Uses heuristics to find the shortest path efficiently by combining actual cost from start with estimated cost to goal.
function aStar(grid, start, end) {
    const openSet = [start];
    const cameFrom = {};
    const gScore = {};
    const fScore = {};
    
    gScore[start] = 0;
    fScore[start] = heuristic(start, end);
    
    while (openSet.length > 0) {
        const current = openSet.reduce((a, b) => 
            fScore[a] < fScore[b] ? a : b);
        
        if (current === end) return reconstructPath(cameFrom, current);
        
        openSet.splice(openSet.indexOf(current), 1);
        
        for (const neighbor of getNeighbors(current, grid)) {
            const tentG = gScore[current] + 1;
            if (tentG < (gScore[neighbor] || Infinity)) {
                cameFrom[neighbor] = current;
                gScore[neighbor] = tentG;
                fScore[neighbor] = tentG + heuristic(neighbor, end);
                if (!openSet.includes(neighbor)) openSet.push(neighbor);
            }
        }
    }
    return null;
}
Dijkstra's Algorithm
Finds the shortest path to all nodes in a network. Ideal when target locations dynamically change or when you need distances to all reachable points.
function dijkstra(graph, start) {
    const distances = {};
    const previous = {};
    const queue = Object.keys(graph);
    
    Object.keys(graph).forEach(node => {
        distances[node] = Infinity;
        previous[node] = null;
    });
    distances[start] = 0;
    
    while (queue.length > 0) {
        const current = queue.reduce((a, b) => 
            distances[a] < distances[b] ? a : b);
        queue.splice(queue.indexOf(current), 1);
        
        if (distances[current] === Infinity) break;
        
        for (const neighbor in graph[current]) {
            const alt = distances[current] + graph[current][neighbor];
            if (alt < distances[neighbor]) {
                distances[neighbor] = alt;
                previous[neighbor] = current;
            }
        }
    }
    return { distances, previous };
}
Breadth-First Search (BFS)
Explores closest nodes first. Perfect for tile-based area highlights like movement range in tactical games and finding shortest path in unweighted graphs.
function bfs(graph, start) {
    const visited = new Set();
    const queue = [start];
    const order = [];
    visited.add(start);
    
    while (queue.length > 0) {
        const vertex = queue.shift();
        order.push(vertex);
        
        for (const neighbor of graph[vertex]) {
            if (!visited.has(neighbor)) {
                visited.add(neighbor);
                queue.push(neighbor);
            }
        }
    }
    return order;
}
Depth-First Search (DFS)
Explores down paths as far as possible before backtracking. Highly useful for maze generation, puzzle validation, and cycle detection in graphs.
function dfs(graph, start, visited = new Set()) {
    visited.add(start);
    const order = [start];
    
    for (const neighbor of graph[start]) {
        if (!visited.has(neighbor)) {
            order.push(...dfs(graph, neighbor, visited));
        }
    }
    return order;
}

// Iterative version
function dfsIterative(graph, start) {
    const visited = new Set();
    const stack = [start];
    const order = [];
    
    while (stack.length > 0) {
        const vertex = stack.pop();
        if (!visited.has(vertex)) {
            visited.add(vertex);
            order.push(vertex);
            for (const neighbor of graph[vertex]) {
                if (!visited.has(neighbor)) stack.push(neighbor);
            }
        }
    }
    return order;
}
Theta* Algorithm
An extension of A* that allows for any-angle pathfinding, preventing characters from looking robotic by moving strictly on grid lines.
function thetaStar(grid, start, end) {
    // Theta* extends A* by allowing any-angle paths
    // Key difference: UpdateVertex considers direct paths
    
    function updateVertex(u, v, g, parent) {
        const newG = gScore[u] + distance(u, v);
        if (newG < gScore[v]) {
            gScore[v] = newG;
            parent[v] = u;
            fScore[v] = gScore[v] + heuristic(v, end);
        }
        
        // Theta* specific: check if path through parent is better
        const p = parent[u];
        if (p && lineOfSight(p, v)) {
            const altG = gScore[p] + distance(p, v);
            if (altG < gScore[v]) {
                gScore[v] = altG;
                parent[v] = p;
                fScore[v] = gScore[v] + heuristic(v, end);
            }
        }
    }
    
    function lineOfSight(p1, p2) {
        // Bresenham's line algorithm for grid visibility
        let x0 = p1.x, y0 = p1.y;
        let x1 = p2.x, y1 = p2.y;
        const dx = Math.abs(x1 - x0), dy = Math.abs(y1 - y0);
        const sx = x0 < x1 ? 1 : -1, sy = y0 < y1 ? 1 : -1;
        let err = dx - dy;
        
        while (true) {
            if (grid[y0][x0] === 1) return false;
            if (x0 === x1 && y0 === y1) break;
            const e2 = 2 * err;
            if (e2 > -dy) { err -= dy; x0 += sx; }
            if (e2 < dx) { err += dx; y0 += sy; }
        }
        return true;
    }
    
    // A* main loop with UpdateVertex modification
    return aStarWithTheta(grid, start, end, updateVertex);
}
📦 SPATIAL PARTITIONING & CULL OPTIMIZATION
Divide 2D/3D space to query nearby objects instantly for collision and rendering
Quadtree
Recursively divides 2D space into four quadrants. Crucial for 2D collision detection and view-culling by reducing the number of pairwise checks.
class QuadTree {
    constructor(boundary, capacity) {
        this.boundary = boundary;
        this.capacity = capacity;
        this.points = [];
        this.divided = false;
    }

    subdivide() {
        const { x, y, w, h } = this.boundary;
        const nw = { x: x, y: y, w: w/2, h: h/2 };
        const ne = { x: x+w/2, y: y, w: w/2, h: h/2 };
        const sw = { x: x, y: y+h/2, w: w/2, h: h/2 };
        const se = { x: x+w/2, y: y+h/2, w: w/2, h: h/2 };
        
        this.northwest = new QuadTree(nw, this.capacity);
        this.northeast = new QuadTree(ne, this.capacity);
        this.southwest = new QuadTree(sw, this.capacity);
        this.southeast = new QuadTree(se, this.capacity);
        this.divided = true;
    }

    insert(point) {
        if (!this.contains(point)) return false;
        if (this.points.length < this.capacity) {
            this.points.push(point);
            return true;
        }
        if (!this.divided) this.subdivide();
        return this.northwest.insert(point) || 
               this.northeast.insert(point) ||
               this.southwest.insert(point) || 
               this.southeast.insert(point);
    }

    query(range, found = []) {
        if (!this.intersects(range)) return found;
        for (const p of this.points) {
            if (range.contains(p)) found.push(p);
        }
        if (this.divided) {
            this.northwest.query(range, found);
            this.northeast.query(range, found);
            this.southwest.query(range, found);
            this.southeast.query(range, found);
        }
        return found;
    }
}
Octree
Recursively divides 3D space into eight octants. Used for 3D engine visibility, voxel rendering, and efficient spatial queries in volumetric data.
class Octree {
    constructor(bounds, maxObjects = 10) {
        this.bounds = bounds;
        this.maxObjects = maxObjects;
        this.objects = [];
        this.nodes = [];
    }

    subdivide() {
        const { x, y, z, w, h, d } = this.bounds;
        const hw = w/2, hh = h/2, hd = d/2;
        
        const octants = [
            {x, y, z, w:hw, h:hh, d:hd},
            {x+hw, y, z, w:hw, h:hh, d:hd},
            {x, y+hh, z, w:hw, h:hh, d:hd},
            {x+hw, y+hh, z, w:hw, h:hh, d:hd},
            {x, y, z+hd, w:hw, h:hh, d:hd},
            {x+hw, y, z+hd, w:hw, h:hh, d:hd},
            {x, y+hh, z+hd, w:hw, h:hh, d:hd},
            {x+hw, y+hh, z+hd, w:hw, h:hh, d:hd}
        ];
        
        this.nodes = octants.map(b => new Octree(b, this.maxObjects));
    }

    insert(object) {
        if (!this.containsBounds(object)) return false;
        if (this.objects.length < this.maxObjects) {
            this.objects.push(object);
            return true;
        }
        if (this.nodes.length === 0) this.subdivide();
        for (const node of this.nodes) {
            if (node.insert(object)) return true;
        }
        return false;
    }

    query(range, found = []) {
        if (!this.intersects(range)) return found;
        for (const obj of this.objects) {
            if (this.pointInBounds(obj, range)) found.push(obj);
        }
        for (const node of this.nodes) {
            node.query(range, found);
        }
        return found;
    }
}
Bounding Volume Hierarchy (BVH)
Organizes objects into a tree structure of nested geometric shapes. Essential for ray tracing and physics broad-phase collision sweeps.
class BVH {
    constructor(objects) {
        this.root = this.build(objects);
    }

    build(objects) {
        if (objects.length === 0) return null;
        if (objects.length === 1) {
            return { 
                bounds: objects[0].bounds, 
                object: objects[0], 
                left: null, 
                right: null 
            };
        }

        const centroid = this.getCentroid(objects);
        objects.sort((a, b) => {
            const ca = this.getCenter(a.bounds);
            const cb = this.getCenter(b.bounds);
            return ca.x - cb.x;
        });

        const mid = Math.floor(objects.length / 2);
        return {
            bounds: this.computeBounds(objects),
            left: this.build(objects.slice(0, mid)),
            right: this.build(objects.slice(mid)),
            object: null
        };
    }

    intersect(ray, node) {
        if (!node) return null;
        if (!this.rayIntersectsBounds(ray, node.bounds)) return null;
        if (node.object) return node.object;
        
        const leftHit = this.intersect(ray, node.left);
        const rightHit = this.intersect(ray, node.right);
        
        if (leftHit && rightHit) {
            return this.closerHit(ray, leftHit, rightHit);
        }
        return leftHit || rightHit;
    }
}
Binary Space Partitioning (BSP)
Historically used in early 3D games like DOOM to render map geometry back-to-front without sorting artifacts using portal-based visibility.
class BSPNode {
    constructor(polygons) {
        this.polygons = polygons;
        this.plane = null;
        this.front = null;
        this.back = null;
    }

    build(polygons) {
        if (polygons.length === 0) return null;

        this.plane = this.selectSplitPlane(polygons);
        const front = [], back = [];

        for (const poly of polygons) {
            const { front: f, back: b } = this.splitPolygon(poly, this.plane);
            if (f) front.push(f);
            if (b) back.push(b);
        }

        this.front = new BSPNode(front).build(front);
        this.back = new BSPNode(back).build(back);
        return this;
    }

    render(viewpoint) {
        if (!this.plane) return;
        
        const side = this.classifyPoint(viewpoint, this.plane);
        
        if (side <= 0) {
            if (this.back) this.back.render(viewpoint);
            this.drawPolygons(this.polygons);
            if (this.front) this.front.render(viewpoint);
        } else {
            if (this.front) this.front.render(viewpoint);
            this.drawPolygons(this.polygons);
            if (this.back) this.back.render(viewpoint);
        }
    }

    classifyPoint(point, plane) {
        return plane.normal.dot(point) - plane.distance;
    }
}
Frustum Culling
Determines what falls inside the camera's field of view so the graphics engine avoids wasting power rendering objects behind the player.
class Frustum {
    constructor(viewProjectionMatrix) {
        this.planes = this.extractPlanes(viewProjectionMatrix);
    }

    extractPlanes(m) {
        return [
            { a: m[3]+m[0], b: m[7]+m[4], c: m[11]+m[8], d: m[15]+m[12] }, // Left
            { a: m[3]-m[0], b: m[7]-m[4], c: m[11]-m[8], d: m[15]-m[12] }, // Right
            { a: m[3]+m[1], b: m[7]+m[5], c: m[11]+m[9], d: m[15]+m[13] }, // Bottom
            { a: m[3]-m[1], b: m[7]-m[5], c: m[11]-m[9], d: m[15]-m[13] }, // Top
            { a: m[3]+m[2], b: m[7]+m[6], c: m[11]+m[10], d: m[15]+m[14] }, // Near
            { a: m[3]-m[2], b: m[7]-m[6], c: m[11]-m[10], d: m[15]-m[14] }  // Far
        ];
    }

    testAABB(aabb) {
        for (const plane of this.planes) {
            const pVertex = this.getPositiveVertex(aabb, plane);
            if (this.distanceToPoint(pVertex, plane) < 0) {
                return false;
            }
        }
        return true;
    }

    getPositiveVertex(aabb, plane) {
        const p = aabb.min.clone();
        if (plane.a >= 0) p.x = aabb.max.x;
        if (plane.b >= 0) p.y = aabb.max.y;
        if (plane.c >= 0) p.z = aabb.max.z;
        return p;
    }

    distanceToPoint(point, plane) {
        return plane.a * point.x + plane.b * point.y + 
               plane.c * point.z + plane.d;
    }
}
💥 PHYSICS & COLLISION DETECTION
Power realism of object movement, gravitational forces, and structural boundaries
Gilbert-Johnson-Keerthi (GJK)
Evaluates the closest distance between convex shapes using Minkowski differences. The foundation of modern 3D collision systems.
function gjk(shapeA, shapeB) {
    const dir = shapeA.center().sub(shapeB.center());
    const simplex = [minkowskiDiff(shapeA, shapeB, dir)];
    
    dir.negate();
    
    for (let i = 0; i < 100; i++) {
        const a = minkowskiDiff(shapeA, shapeB, dir);
        
        if (a.dot(dir) < 0) return false;
        
        simplex.push(a);
        
        if (handleSimplex(simplex, dir)) return true;
    }
    return false;
}

function handleSimplex(simplex, dir) {
    const a = simplex[simplex.length - 1];
    
    if (simplex.length === 1) {
        dir.copy(a).negate();
        return false;
    }
    
    if (simplex.length === 2) {
        const b = simplex[0];
        const ab = b.sub(a);
        const ao = a.negate();
        
        if (ab.dot(ao) > 0) {
            dir.copy(ab.cross(ao).cross(ab));
        } else {
            simplex.splice(0, 1);
            dir.copy(ao);
        }
        return false;
    }
    
    // Triangle case
    const b = simplex[1], c = simplex[0];
    const ab = b.sub(a), ac = c.sub(a);
    const ao = a.negate();
    
    const abc = ab.cross(ac);
    
    if (abc.cross(ac).dot(ao) > 0) {
        simplex.splice(1, 1);
        dir.copy(ac.cross(abc));
        return false;
    }
    
    if (ab.cross(abc).dot(ao) > 0) {
        simplex.splice(0, 1);
        dir.copy(abc.cross(ab));
        return false;
    }
    
    return true;
}
Sutherland-Hodgman Algorithm
Clips polygons against boundaries. Used to pinpoint precise intersection contact points for rigid bodies and collision response.
function sutherlandHodgman(subjectPolygon, clipPolygon) {
    const output = [...subjectPolygon];
    
    for (let i = 0; i < clipPolygon.length; i++) {
        if (output.length === 0) return [];
        
        const input = [...output];
        output.length = 0;
        
        const edgeStart = clipPolygon[i];
        const edgeEnd = clipPolygon[(i + 1) % clipPolygon.length];
        
        for (let j = 0; j < input.length; j++) {
            const current = input[j];
            const previous = input[(j + input.length - 1) % input.length];
            
            if (isInside(current, edgeStart, edgeEnd)) {
                if (!isInside(previous, edgeStart, edgeEnd)) {
                    output.push(intersection(previous, current, edgeStart, edgeEnd));
                }
                output.push(current);
            } else if (isInside(previous, edgeStart, edgeEnd)) {
                output.push(intersection(previous, current, edgeStart, edgeEnd));
            }
        }
    }
    return output;
}

function isInside(point, edgeStart, edgeEnd) {
    return (edgeEnd.x - edgeStart.x) * (point.y - edgeStart.y) -
           (edgeEnd.y - edgeStart.y) * (point.x - edgeStart.x) >= 0;
}

function intersection(p1, p2, p3, p4) {
    const x1 = p1.x, y1 = p1.y, x2 = p2.x, y2 = p2.y;
    const x3 = p3.x, y3 = p3.y, x4 = p4.x, y4 = p4.y;
    
    const denom = (x1 - x2) * (y3 - y4) - (y1 - y2) * (x3 - x4);
    const t = ((x1 - x3) * (y3 - y4) - (y1 - y3) * (x3 - x4)) / denom;
    
    return { x: x1 + t * (x2 - x1), y: y1 + t * (y2 - y1) };
}
Verlet Integration
A numerical integration technique to calculate physics states. Ideal for cloth, rope, and soft-body particle simulations.
class Particle {
    constructor(x, y, mass = 1) {
        this.x = x;
        this.y = y;
        this.prevX = x;
        this.prevY = y;
        this.ax = 0;
        this.ay = 0;
        this.mass = mass;
        this.pinned = false;
    }

    applyForce(fx, fy) {
        this.ax += fx / this.mass;
        this.ay += fy / this.mass;
    }

    update(dt) {
        if (this.pinned) return;
        
        const vx = this.x - this.prevX;
        const vy = this.y - this.prevY;
        
        this.prevX = this.x;
        this.prevY = this.y;
        
        this.x += vx * 0.99 + this.ax * dt * dt;
        this.y += vy * 0.99 + this.ay * dt * dt;
        
        this.ax = 0;
        this.ay = 0;
    }
}

class Constraint {
    constructor(p1, p2, length) {
        this.p1 = p1;
        this.p2 = p2;
        this.length = length || Math.hypot(p2.x - p1.x, p2.y - p1.y);
    }

    solve() {
        const dx = this.p2.x - this.p1.x;
        const dy = this.p2.y - this.p1.y;
        const dist = Math.hypot(dx, dy);
        const diff = (this.length - dist) / dist * 0.5;
        
        if (!this.p1.pinned) {
            this.p1.x -= dx * diff;
            this.p1.y -= dy * diff;
        }
        if (!this.p2.pinned) {
            this.p2.x += dx * diff;
            this.p2.y += dy * diff;
        }
    }
}
Euler/Runge-Kutta Integration
Solves physics differential equations over time to compute positions from acceleration and velocity for accurate simulation.
function eulerIntegration(state, derivative, dt) {
    return {
        x: state.x + derivative.dx * dt,
        v: state.v + derivative.dv * dt
    };
}

function rungeKutta4(state, derivatives, dt) {
    const k1 = derivatives(state);
    
    const s2 = { 
        x: state.x + k1.dx * dt/2, 
        v: state.v + k1.dv * dt/2 
    };
    const k2 = derivatives(s2);
    
    const s3 = { 
        x: state.x + k2.dx * dt/2, 
        v: state.v + k2.dv * dt/2 
    };
    const k3 = derivatives(s3);
    
    const s4 = { 
        x: state.x + k3.dx * dt, 
        v: state.v + k3.dv * dt 
    };
    const k4 = derivatives(s4);
    
    return {
        x: state.x + (k1.dx + 2*k2.dx + 2*k3.dx + k4.dx) * dt/6,
        v: state.v + (k1.dv + 2*k2.dv + 2*k3.dv + k4.dv) * dt/6
    };
}

// Projectile motion example
const derivatives = (state) => ({
    dx: state.v,
    dv: -9.81  // gravity
});

let state = { x: 0, v: 50 };  // initial velocity
for (let i = 0; i < 100; i++) {
    state = rungeKutta4(state, derivatives, 0.01);
}
Separating Axis Theorem (SAT)
Checks for overlaps between flat geometric shapes by projecting them onto 2D lines. Highly popular for simple 2D collision engines.
function satCollision(shapeA, shapeB) {
    const axes = [...getAxes(shapeA), ...getAxes(shapeB)];
    let minOverlap = Infinity;
    let smallestAxis = null;

    for (const axis of axes) {
        const projA = project(shapeA, axis);
        const projB = project(shapeB, axis);
        
        const overlap = Math.min(projA.max - projB.min, projB.max - projA.min);
        
        if (overlap <= 0) return null;  // Separating axis found
        
        if (overlap < minOverlap) {
            minOverlap = overlap;
            smallestAxis = axis;
        }
    }

    // Ensure normal points from A to B
    const centerA = getCenter(shapeA);
    const centerB = getCenter(shapeB);
    const d = centerB.sub(centerA);
    if (d.dot(smallestAxis) < 0) {
        smallestAxis.negate();
    }

    return { overlap: minOverlap, normal: smallestAxis };
}

function getAxes(shape) {
    const axes = [];
    for (let i = 0; i < shape.vertices.length; i++) {
        const j = (i + 1) % shape.vertices.length;
        const edge = shape.vertices[j].sub(shape.vertices[i]);
        axes.push({ x: -edge.y, y: edge.x }.normalize());
    }
    return axes;
}

function project(shape, axis) {
    let min = Infinity, max = -Infinity;
    for (const v of shape.vertices) {
        const proj = v.dot(axis);
        min = Math.min(min, proj);
        max = Math.max(max, proj);
    }
    return { min, max };
}
🌍 PROCEDURAL CONTENT GENERATION (PCG)
Endlessly generate terrain, textures, maps, and structures without hard-coding
Perlin Noise / Simplex Noise
Generates smooth, organic pseudo-random values. Ideal for creating natural-looking heightmaps, clouds, and texturing in game worlds.
class PerlinNoise {
    constructor(seed = Math.random()) {
        this.perm = this.generatePermutation(seed);
    }

    generatePermutation(seed) {
        const p = Array.from({length: 256}, (_, i) => i);
        for (let i = 255; i > 0; i--) {
            seed = (seed * 16807) % 2147483647;
            const j = Math.floor((seed / 2147483647) * (i + 1));
            [p[i], p[j]] = [p[j], p[i]];
        }
        return [...p, ...p];
    }

    fade(t) { return t * t * t * (t * (t * 6 - 15) + 10); }

    lerp(a, b, t) { return a + t * (b - a); }

    grad(hash, x, y) {
        const h = hash & 3;
        const u = h < 2 ? x : y;
        const v = h < 2 ? y : x;
        return ((h & 1) ? -u : u) + ((h & 2) ? -v : v);
    }

    noise2D(x, y) {
        const X = Math.floor(x) & 255;
        const Y = Math.floor(y) & 255;
        x -= Math.floor(x);
        y -= Math.floor(y);
        const u = this.fade(x);
        const v = this.fade(y);
        const A = this.perm[X] + Y, B = this.perm[X + 1] + Y;
        return this.lerp(
            this.lerp(this.grad(this.perm[A], x, y), this.grad(this.perm[B], x-1, y), u),
            this.lerp(this.grad(this.perm[A+1], x, y-1), this.grad(this.perm[B+1], x-1, y-1), u),
            v
        );
    }

    octaveNoise(x, y, octaves = 4, persistence = 0.5) {
        let total = 0, frequency = 1, amplitude = 1, maxValue = 0;
        for (let i = 0; i < octaves; i++) {
            total += this.noise2D(x * frequency, y * frequency) * amplitude;
            maxValue += amplitude;
            amplitude *= persistence;
            frequency *= 2;
        }
        return total / maxValue;
    }
}
Cellular Automata
Generates cave systems or fluid simulations using localized grid-neighbor rules, often based on Conway's Game of Life.
function cellularAutomata(width, height, fillProb = 0.45) {
    let grid = Array(height).fill(null).map(() =>
        Array(width).fill(null).map(() => Math.random() < fillProb ? 1 : 0)
    );

    function countNeighbors(g, x, y) {
        let count = 0;
        for (let dy = -1; dy <= 1; dy++) {
            for (let dx = -1; dx <= 1; dx++) {
                if (dx === 0 && dy === 0) continue;
                const nx = x + dx, ny = y + dy;
                if (nx < 0 || nx >= width || ny < 0 || ny >= height) count++;
                else count += g[ny][nx];
            }
        }
        return count;
    }

    function simulate(steps = 5) {
        for (let s = 0; s < steps; s++) {
            const newGrid = grid.map(row => [...row]);
            for (let y = 0; y < height; y++) {
                for (let x = 0; x < width; x++) {
                    const neighbors = countNeighbors(grid, x, y);
                    if (neighbors > 4) newGrid[y][x] = 1;
                    else if (neighbors < 4) newGrid[y][x] = 0;
                }
            }
            grid = newGrid;
        }
        return grid;
    }

    return { grid, simulate };
}

// Game of Life
function gameOfLifeStep(grid) {
    const h = grid.length, w = grid[0].length;
    return grid.map((row, y) => row.map((cell, x) => {
        let neighbors = 0;
        for (let dy = -1; dy <= 1; dy++)
            for (let dx = -1; dx <= 1; dx++)
                if (dx || dy) neighbors += grid[y+dy]?.[x+dx] || 0;
        return cell ? (neighbors === 2 || neighbors === 3 ? 1 : 0) : (neighbors === 3 ? 1 : 0);
    }));
}
Wave Function Collapse (WFC)
Procedurally populates a grid with tiles based on specific compatibility rules, mimicking infinite tile placement or building design.
class WFC {
    constructor(tiles, rules, width, height) {
        this.tiles = tiles;
        this.rules = rules;
        this.width = width;
        this.height = height;
        this.grid = Array(height).fill(null).map(() =>
            Array(width).fill(null).map(() => new Set(tiles))
        );
    }

    getLowestEntropy() {
        let min = Infinity, best = null;
        for (let y = 0; y < this.height; y++) {
            for (let x = 0; x < this.width; x++) {
                const options = this.grid[y][x].size;
                if (options > 1 && options < min) {
                    min = options;
                    best = { x, y };
                }
            }
        }
        return best;
    }

    collapse(x, y) {
        const options = [...this.grid[y][x]];
        const chosen = options[Math.floor(Math.random() * options.length)];
        this.grid[y][x] = new Set([chosen]);
        this.propagate(x, y);
    }

    propagate(x, y) {
        const stack = [[x, y]];
        while (stack.length > 0) {
            const [cx, cy] = stack.pop();
            const current = [...this.grid[cy][cx]];
            for (const [dx, dy, dir] of [[0,1,'S'],[0,-1,'N'],[1,0,'E'],[-1,0,'W']]) {
                const nx = cx + dx, ny = cy + dy;
                if (nx < 0 || nx >= this.width || ny < 0 || ny >= this.height) continue;
                const before = this.grid[ny][nx].size;
                this.grid[ny][nx] = new Set([...this.grid[ny][nx]].filter(t =>
                    current.some(ct => this.rules[ct]?.[dir]?.includes(t))
                ));
                if (this.grid[ny][nx].size < before) stack.push([nx, ny]);
            }
        }
    }

    solve() {
        let cell;
        while ((cell = this.getLowestEntropy())) {
            this.collapse(cell.x, cell.y);
        }
        return this.grid.map(row => row.map(cell => [...cell][0]));
    }
}
Marching Cubes
Generates smooth 3D polygon meshes out of raw, grid-based volumetric density points. Used heavily in voxel and destructible terrain games.
const EDGE_TABLE = [0x0,0x109,0x203,0x30a,0x406,0x50f,0x605,0x70c,...];
const TRI_TABLE = [[-1,-1,-1,-1,-1,-1,-1],[0,8,3,-1,-1,-1,-1],...];

function marchingCubes(field, size, threshold = 0) {
    const vertices = [];
    const normals = [];

    for (let z = 0; z < size-1; z++) {
        for (let y = 0; y < size-1; y++) {
            for (let x = 0; x < size-1; x++) {
                const cube = [
                    field[z][y][x], field[z][y][x+1],
                    field[z][y+1][x], field[z][y+1][x+1],
                    field[z+1][y][x], field[z+1][y][x+1],
                    field[z+1][y+1][x], field[z+1][y+1][x+1]
                ];

                let cubeIndex = 0;
                for (let i = 0; i < 8; i++) {
                    if (cube[i] > threshold) cubeIndex |= (1 << i);
                }

                const edges = EDGE_TABLE[cubeIndex];
                if (edges === 0) continue;

                const edgeVerts = new Array(12);
                for (let i = 0; i < 12; i++) {
                    if (edges & (1 << i)) {
                        const [a, b] = getEdgeVertices(i);
                        const t = (threshold - cube[a]) / (cube[b] - cube[a]);
                        edgeVerts[i] = interpolate(x, y, z, a, b, t, size);
                    }
                }

                for (let i = 0; TRI_TABLE[cubeIndex][i] !== -1; i += 3) {
                    vertices.push(
                        edgeVerts[TRI_TABLE[cubeIndex][i]],
                        edgeVerts[TRI_TABLE[cubeIndex][i+1]],
                        edgeVerts[TRI_TABLE[cubeIndex][i+2]]
                    );
                }
            }
        }
    }
    return vertices;
}
Delaunay Triangulation & Voronoi
Connects random points into networks of triangles or cells. Used for regional map generation and breaking glass/shattering mechanics.
function delaunayTriangulate(points) {
    const triangles = [];
    const superTriangle = [
        { x: -10000, y: -10000 },
        { x: 10000, y: -10000 },
        { x: 0, y: 10000 }
    ];
    triangles.push(superTriangle);

    for (const point of points) {
        const badTriangles = triangles.filter(t => isInCircumcircle(point, t));
        const polygon = [];
        
        for (const tri of badTriangles) {
            for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++) {
                const edge = [tri[i], tri[(i+1)%3]];
                const shared = badTriangles.some(t => 
                    t !== tri && t.some(p => 
                        p.x === edge[1].x && p.y === edge[1].y
                    ) && t.some(p => 
                        p.x === edge[0].x && p.y === edge[0].y
                    )
                );
                if (!shared) polygon.push(edge);
            }
        }
        
        badTriangles.forEach(t => triangles.splice(triangles.indexOf(t), 1));
        polygon.forEach(edge => triangles.push([edge[0], edge[1], point]));
    }

    return triangles.filter(t => !t.some(p => 
        superTriangle.some(sp => sp.x === p.x && sp.y === p.y)
    ));
}

function voronoiFromDelaunay(triangles, bounds) {
    const circumcenters = triangles.map(t => getCircumcenter(t));
    return circumcenters;
}

function isInCircumcircle(point, triangle) {
    const [a, b, c] = triangle;
    const ax = a.x - point.x, ay = a.y - point.y;
    const bx = b.x - point.x, by = b.y - point.y;
    const cx = c.x - point.x, cy = c.y - point.y;
    const det = (ax*ax + ay*ay) * (bx*cy - cx*by) -
                (bx*bx + by*by) * (ax*cy - cx*ay) +
                (cx*cx + cy*cy) * (ax*by - bx*ay);
    return det > 0;
}
L-Systems (Lindenmayer Systems)
Uses string rewriting rules to procedurally generate complex branching plant life, trees, and fractal structures.
class LSystem {
    constructor(axiom, rules, angle = 25) {
        this.axiom = axiom;
        this.rules = rules;
        this.angle = angle;
        this.current = axiom;
    }

    iterate(n = 1) {
        for (let i = 0; i < n; i++) {
            let next = '';
            for (const char of this.current) {
                next += this.rules[char] || char;
            }
            this.current = next;
        }
        return this.current;
    }

    draw(ctx, x, y, length = 10, startAngle = -90) {
        const stack = [];
        let angle = startAngle;
        let px = x, py = y;

        ctx.beginPath();
        ctx.moveTo(x, y);

        for (const char of this.current) {
            switch (char) {
                case 'F':
                    const nx = px + length * Math.cos(angle * Math.PI / 180);
                    const ny = py + length * Math.sin(angle * Math.PI / 180);
                    ctx.lineTo(nx, ny);
                    px = nx; py = ny;
                    break;
                case '+': angle += this.angle; break;
                case '-': angle -= this.angle; break;
                case '[':
                    stack.push({ x: px, y: py, angle });
                    length *= 0.7;
                    break;
                case ']':
                    const state = stack.pop();
                    px = state.x; py = state.y; angle = state.angle;
                    length /= 0.7;
                    ctx.moveTo(px, py);
                    break;
            }
        }
        ctx.stroke();
    }
}

// Examples
const tree = new LSystem('F', { 'F': 'FF+[+F-F-F]-[-F+F+F]' }, 22.5);
const koch = new LSystem('F', { 'F': 'F+F-F-F+F' }, 90);
const sierpinski = new LSystem('F-G-G', { 'F': 'F-G+F+G-F', 'G': 'GG' }, 120);
🧠 ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE & DECISION MAKING
Dictate how enemies, allies, and environments behave for deep gameplay
Behavior Trees
Structured hierarchical trees that execute actions based on preconditions. The modern industry standard for complex enemy AI.
class BTNode {
    tick blackboard { return 'SUCCESS'; }
}

class Selector extends BTNode {
    constructor(children) { super(); this.children = children; }
    tick(bb) {
        for (const child of this.children) {
            const result = child.tick(bb);
            if (result !== 'FAILURE') return result;
        }
        return 'FAILURE';
    }
}

class Sequence extends BTNode {
    constructor(children) { super(); this.children = children; }
    tick(bb) {
        for (const child of this.children) {
            const result = child.tick(bb);
            if (result !== 'SUCCESS') return result;
        }
        return 'SUCCESS';
    }
}

class Condition extends BTNode {
    constructor(check) { super(); this.check = check; }
    tick(bb) { return this.check(bb) ? 'SUCCESS' : 'FAILURE'; }
}

class Action extends BTNode {
    constructor(action) { super(); this.action = action; }
    tick(bb) { return this.action(bb); }
}

// Example: Enemy AI
const enemyTree = new Selector([
    new Sequence([
        new Condition(bb => bb.health < 20),
        new Action(bb => { bb.state = 'FLEE'; return 'SUCCESS'; })
    ]),
    new Sequence([
        new Condition(bb => bb.enemyVisible),
        new Action(bb => { bb.state = 'ATTACK'; return 'SUCCESS'; })
    ]),
    new Action(bb => { bb.state = 'PATROL'; return 'SUCCESS'; })
]);
Finite State Machine (FSM)
Transition-based logic models where an agent switches between predefined modes like Idle, Patrol, and Attack.
class FSM {
    constructor(initialState) {
        this.state = initialState;
        this.transitions = {};
        this.states = {};
    }

    addState(name, onEnter, onUpdate, onExit) {
        this.states[name] = { onEnter, onUpdate, onExit };
    }

    addTransition(from, to, condition) {
        if (!this.transitions[from]) this.transitions[from] = [];
        this.transitions[from].push({ to, condition });
    }

    update(context) {
        if (this.states[this.state]?.onUpdate) {
            this.states[this.state].onUpdate(context);
        }

        const transitions = this.transitions[this.state] || [];
        for (const { to, condition } of transitions) {
            if (condition(context)) {
                this.transition(to, context);
                break;
            }
        }
    }

    transition(newState, context) {
        if (this.states[this.state]?.onExit) {
            this.states[this.state].onExit(context);
        }
        const oldState = this.state;
        this.state = newState;
        if (this.states[this.state]?.onEnter) {
            this.states[this.state].onEnter(context);
        }
        console.log(`State: ${oldState} → ${newState}`);
    }
}

// Usage
const ai = new FSM('IDLE');
ai.addState('IDLE', 
    () => console.log('Standing still'),
    (ctx) => {},
    () => {}
);
ai.addState('PATROL',
    () => console.log('Starting patrol'),
    (ctx) => ctx.moveAlongPath(),
    () => {}
);
ai.addState('ATTACK',
    () => console.log('Engaging enemy'),
    (ctx) => ctx.attack(),
    () => {}
);
ai.addTransition('IDLE', 'PATROL', ctx => ctx.enemyVisible === false);
ai.addTransition('PATROL', 'ATTACK', ctx => ctx.enemyVisible);
Craig Reynolds' Steering Behaviors
Simple mathematical rules that calculate vectors to make entities smoothly seek, flee, wander, or follow paths.
class Vector2 {
    constructor(x=0, y=0) { this.x = x; this.y = y; }
    add(v) { return new Vector2(this.x+v.x, this.y+v.y); }
    sub(v) { return new Vector2(this.x-v.x, this.y-v.y); }
    mul(s) { return new Vector2(this.x*s, this.y*s); }
    mag() { return Math.hypot(this.x, this.y); }
    normalize() { const m = this.mag(); return m ? this.mul(1/m) : new Vector2(); }
    limit(max) { return this.mag() > max ? this.normalize().mul(max) : this; }
}

class Steering {
    static seek(entity, target) {
        const desired = target.sub(entity.pos).normalize().mul(entity.maxSpeed);
        return desired.sub(entity.vel);
    }

    static flee(entity, target) {
        return Steering.seek(entity, target).mul(-1);
    }

    static wander(entity, wanderDist = 100, wanderRadius = 25) {
        const circleCenter = entity.vel.normalize().mul(wanderDist);
        const angle = Math.random() * Math.PI * 2;
        const displacement = new Vector2(
            Math.cos(angle) * wanderRadius,
            Math.sin(angle) * wanderRadius
        );
        return circleCenter.add(displacement).sub(entity.vel);
    }

    static arrive(entity, target, slowRadius = 100) {
        const desired = target.sub(entity.pos);
        const dist = desired.mag();
        let speed = entity.maxSpeed;
        if (dist < slowRadius) speed *= dist / slowRadius;
        return desired.normalize().mul(speed).sub(entity.vel);
    }

    static pursuit(entity, target) {
        const prediction = target.pos.add(target.vel.mul(30));
        return Steering.seek(entity, prediction);
    }

    static evade(entity, threat) {
        const prediction = threat.pos.add(threat.vel.mul(30));
        return Steering.flee(entity, prediction);
    }
}
Flocking Boids Algorithm
Simulates natural group movement like schools of fish or birds using separation, alignment, and cohesion rules.
class Boid {
    constructor(x, y) {
        this.pos = { x, y };
        this.vel = { x: (Math.random()-0.5)*4, y: (Math.random()-0.5)*4 };
        this.acc = { x: 0, y: 0 };
        this.maxSpeed = 4;
        this.maxForce = 0.1;
    }

    separate(boids) {
        let steer = { x: 0, y: 0 };
        let count = 0;
        for (const other of boids) {
            const d = Math.hypot(this.pos.x-other.pos.x, this.pos.y-other.pos.y);
            if (d > 0 && d < 25) {
                steer.x += this.pos.x - other.pos.x;
                steer.y += this.pos.y - other.pos.y;
                count++;
            }
        }
        if (count > 0) { steer.x /= count; steer.y /= count; }
        return steer;
    }

    align(boids) {
        let sum = { x: 0, y: 0 };
        let count = 0;
        for (const other of boids) {
            const d = Math.hypot(this.pos.x-other.pos.x, this.pos.y-other.pos.y);
            if (d > 0 && d < 50) {
                sum.x += other.vel.x; sum.y += other.vel.y;
                count++;
            }
        }
        if (count > 0) { sum.x /= count; sum.y /= count; }
        return { x: sum.x - this.vel.x, y: sum.y - this.vel.y };
    }

    cohesion(boids) {
        let sum = { x: 0, y: 0 };
        let count = 0;
        for (const other of boids) {
            const d = Math.hypot(this.pos.x-other.pos.x, this.pos.y-other.pos.y);
            if (d > 0 && d < 50) {
                sum.x += other.pos.x; sum.y += other.pos.y;
                count++;
            }
        }
        if (count > 0) { sum.x /= count; sum.y /= count; }
        return { x: sum.x-this.pos.x, y: sum.y-this.pos.y };
    }

    flock(boids) {
        const sep = this.separate(boids);
        const ali = this.align(boids);
        const coh = this.cohesion(boids);
        this.acc.x += sep.x + ali.x + coh.x;
        this.acc.y += sep.y + ali.y + coh.y;
    }
}
Minimax & Alpha-Beta Pruning
Used in turn-based strategy games like Chess to evaluate future moves and prune paths the player would easily block.
function minimax(node, depth, isMaximizing, alpha, beta) {
    if (depth === 0 || node.isTerminal()) {
        return node.evaluate();
    }

    if (isMaximizing) {
        let maxEval = -Infinity;
        for (const child of node.children()) {
            const eval = minimax(child, depth-1, false, alpha, beta);
            maxEval = Math.max(maxEval, eval);
            alpha = Math.max(alpha, eval);
            if (beta <= alpha) break;  // Beta cutoff
        }
        return maxEval;
    } else {
        let minEval = Infinity;
        for (const child of node.children()) {
            const eval = minimax(child, depth-1, true, alpha, beta);
            minEval = Math.min(minEval, eval);
            beta = Math.min(beta, eval);
            if (beta <= alpha) break;  // Alpha cutoff
        }
        return minEval;
    }
}

function findBestMove(board, depth) {
    let bestMove = null;
    let bestValue = -Infinity;
    const alpha = -Infinity, beta = Infinity;

    for (const move of board.getLegalMoves()) {
        const newBoard = board.makeMove(move);
        const value = minimax(newBoard, depth-1, false, alpha, beta);
        if (value > bestValue) {
            bestValue = value;
            bestMove = move;
        }
    }
    return bestMove;
}

// Connect-4 example
const board = {
    grid: Array(6).fill(null).map(() => Array(7).fill(0)),
    currentPlayer: 1,
    getLegalMoves() {
        return this.grid[0].map((c, i) => c === 0 ? i : -1).filter(i => i >= 0);
    },
    evaluate() { return Math.random() * 2 - 1; }
};
Utility AI
Calculates a mathematical score for multiple potential actions based on the current environment and chooses the highest value.
class UtilityAI {
    constructor() {
        this.actions = [];
    }

    addAction(name, utilityFn, action) {
        this.actions.push({ name, utilityFn, action });
    }

    update(context) {
        let bestAction = null;
        let bestScore = -Infinity;

        for (const { name, utilityFn, action } of this.actions) {
            const score = utilityFn(context);
            if (score > bestScore) {
                bestScore = score;
                bestAction = { name, action, score };
            }
        }

        return bestAction;
    }
}

// Example: NPC decision making
const ai = new UtilityAI();

ai.addAction('Flee',
    ctx => ctx.health < 20 ? 100 : 0,
    ctx => console.log('Running away!')
);

ai.addAction('Attack',
    ctx => ctx.enemyVisible ? ctx.weaponPower * ctx.health / 100 : 0,
    ctx => console.log('Attacking enemy!')
);

ai.addAction('Heal',
    ctx => ctx.health < 50 ? (100 - ctx.health) : 0,
    ctx => console.log('Using health potion!')
);

ai.addAction('Explore',
    ctx => ctx.boredom * 0.5,
    ctx => console.log('Exploring area...')
);

// Context determines behavior
const context = { health: 30, enemyVisible: true, weaponPower: 80, boredom: 20 };
const decision = ai.update(context);
console.log(`Decision: ${decision.name} (score: ${decision.score.toFixed(1)})`);
decision.action(context);
🎨 RENDERING & COMPUTER GRAPHICS
Mathematical processes that dictate how pixels, lighting, and textures are calculated
Ray Tracing / Ray Casting
Projects rays out into a scene to track line-of-sight intersection points. Used for advanced physics, lighting, and rendering.
function rayCast(origin, direction, objects) {
    let closest = null;
    let minDist = Infinity;

    for (const obj of objects) {
        const t = rayIntersect(origin, direction, obj);
        if (t !== null && t < minDist) {
            minDist = t;
            closest = obj;
        }
    }

    return closest ? {
        point: origin.add(direction.mul(minDist)),
        normal: getNormal(closest),
        distance: minDist,
        object: closest
    } : null;
}

function rayIntersectSphere(origin, dir, sphere) {
    const oc = origin.sub(sphere.center);
    const a = dir.dot(dir);
    const b = 2 * oc.dot(dir);
    const c = oc.dot(oc) - sphere.radius * sphere.radius;
    const discriminant = b * b - 4 * a * c;
    if (discriminant < 0) return null;
    return (-b - Math.sqrt(discriminant)) / (2 * a);
}

function rayTraceScene(origin, dir, scene, bounces = 3) {
    const hit = rayCast(origin, dir, scene.objects);
    if (!hit || bounces === 0) return scene.background;
    const mat = hit.object.material;
    const lightDir = scene.light.sub(hit.point).normalize();
    const diffuse = Math.max(0, hit.normal.dot(lightDir));
    const reflected = dir.sub(hit.normal.mul(2*dir.dot(hit.normal)));
    const refl = rayTraceScene(hit.point, reflected, scene, bounces-1);
    return mat.color.mul(diffuse*mat.kd).add(refl.mul(mat.ks));
}
Bresenham's Line Algorithm
Determines which pixels on a 2D raster grid should be selected to draw a straight line between two points.
function bresenhamLine(x0, y0, x1, y1) {
    const pixels = [];
    let dx = Math.abs(x1 - x0);
    let dy = Math.abs(y1 - y0);
    let sx = x0 < x1 ? 1 : -1;
    let sy = y0 < y1 ? 1 : -1;
    let err = dx - dy;

    while (true) {
        pixels.push({ x: x0, y: y0 });
        if (x0 === x1 && y0 === y1) break;
        const e2 = 2 * err;
        if (e2 > -dy) { err -= dy; x0 += sx; }
        if (e2 < dx) { err += dx; y0 += sy; }
    }
    return pixels;
}

// Wu's anti-aliased line
function wuLine(x0, y0, x1, y1) {
    const pixels = [];
    let dx = x1-x0, dy = y1-y0;
    let gradient = dx === 0 ? 1 : dy/dx;
    let x = Math.round(x0), y = y0;
    for (; x <= Math.round(x1); x++) {
        pixels.push({x, y: Math.floor(y), a: 1-(y%1)});
        pixels.push({x, y: Math.ceil(y), a: y%1});
        y += gradient;
    }
    return pixels;
}
Flood Fill
Determines and alters the area connected to a specific node. Essential for map design tools and paint bucket mechanics.
function floodFillStack(grid, x, y, newColor) {
    const oldColor = grid[y][x];
    if (oldColor === newColor) return grid;
    const stack = [[x, y]];
    const rows = grid.length, cols = grid[0].length;

    while (stack.length > 0) {
        const [cx, cy] = stack.pop();
        if (cx < 0 || cx >= cols || cy < 0 || cy >= rows) continue;
        if (grid[cy][cx] !== oldColor) continue;
        grid[cy][cx] = newColor;
        stack.push([cx+1,cy],[cx-1,cy],[cx,cy+1],[cx,cy-1]);
    }
    return grid;
}

// Scanline flood fill (most efficient)
function floodFillScanline(grid, x, y, newColor) {
    const oldColor = grid[y][x];
    if (oldColor === newColor) return grid;
    const stack = [[x, y]];
    while (stack.length > 0) {
        let [cx, cy] = stack.pop();
        let lx = cx;
        while (lx >= 0 && grid[cy][lx] === oldColor) lx--;
        lx++;
        let spanAbove = false, spanBelow = false;
        while (lx < grid[0].length && grid[cy][lx] === oldColor) {
            grid[cy][lx] = newColor;
            if (!spanAbove && cy > 0 && grid[cy-1][lx] === oldColor) {
                stack.push([lx, cy-1]); spanAbove = true;
            } else if (spanAbove && grid[cy-1]?.[lx] !== oldColor) spanAbove = false;
            if (!spanBelow && cy < grid.length-1 && grid[cy+1][lx] === oldColor) {
                stack.push([lx, cy+1]); spanBelow = true;
            } else if (spanBelow && grid[cy+1]?.[lx] !== oldColor) spanBelow = false;
            lx++;
        }
    }
    return grid;
}
Mipmapping
Generates progressively lower-resolution textures to reduce memory and prevent visual aliasing when items are far away.
class Mipmap {
    constructor(baseTexture) {
        this.levels = [baseTexture];
        this.generateLevels(baseTexture);
    }

    generateLevels(texture) {
        while (texture.width > 1 && texture.height > 1) {
            texture = this.downsample(texture);
            this.levels.push(texture);
        }
    }

    downsample(tex) {
        const w = Math.max(1, Math.floor(tex.width / 2));
        const h = Math.max(1, Math.floor(tex.height / 2));
        const newTex = new Uint8Array(w * h * 4);
        for (let y = 0; y < h; y++) {
            for (let x = 0; x < w; x++) {
                const si = (y*2*tex.width + x*2) * 4;
                const di = (y*w + x) * 4;
                for (let c = 0; c < 4; c++) {
                    newTex[di+c] = Math.round((
                        tex.data[si+c] + tex.data[si+4+c] +
                        tex.data[si+tex.width*4+c] + tex.data[si+tex.width*4+4+c]
                    ) / 4);
                }
            }
        }
        return { width: w, height: h, data: newTex };
    }

    getLevel(distance, screenWidth) {
        const d = Math.max(0, Math.log2(screenWidth / distance));
        const level = Math.min(Math.floor(d), this.levels.length - 1);
        return this.levels[level];
    }

    textureMemory() {
        let total = 0;
        for (const level of this.levels) {
            total += level.width * level.height * 4;
        }
        return total;
    }
}
De Casteljau's Algorithm
Evaluates Bezier curves dynamically. Used for smooth paths for camera animations and UI curves.
function deCasteljau(points, t) {
    let temp = points.map(p => ({...p}));

    while (temp.length > 1) {
        const next = [];
        for (let i = 0; i < temp.length - 1; i++) {
            next.push({
                x: (1-t)*temp[i].x + t*temp[i+1].x,
                y: (1-t)*temp[i].y + t*temp[i+1].y
            });
        }
        temp = next;
    }
    return temp[0];
}

function evalBezier(controlPoints, segments = 50) {
    const curve = [];
    for (let i = 0; i <= segments; i++) {
        const t = i / segments;
        curve.push(deCasteljau(controlPoints, t));
    }
    return curve;
}

// Cubic Bezier (most common: CSS animation curves)
function cubicBezier(p0, p1, p2, p3, t) {
    const mt = 1-t;
    return {
        x: mt*mt*mt*p0.x + 3*mt*mt*t*p1.x + 3*mt*t*t*p2.x + t*t*t*p3.x,
        y: mt*mt*mt*p0.y + 3*mt*mt*t*p1.y + 3*mt*t*t*p2.y + t*t*t*p3.y
    };
}

// Split curve at t (for adaptive subdivision)
function splitBezier(points, t) {
    const left = [points[0]], right = [points[points.length-1]];
    let temp = [...points];
    while (temp.length > 1) {
        left.push(temp[1]);
        right.unshift(temp[temp.length-2]);
        const next = [];
        for (let i = 0; i < temp.length - 1; i++) {
            next.push({
                x: (1-t)*temp[i].x + t*temp[i+1].x,
                y: (1-t)*temp[i].y + t*temp[i+1].y
            });
        }
        temp = next;
    }
    return { left, right };
}
⚙️ MATH, OPTIMIZATION & NETWORK HANDLING
Behind-the-scenes algorithms that keep the frame rate high and the experience seamless
Fast Inverse Square Root
A historic bit-level hacking formula used to normalize vectors incredibly fast — vital for light-angle processing.
function fastInvSqrt(x) {
    let i = new Float32Array(1);
    let y = new Float32Array(1);

    i[0] = x;
    i[0] = 0x5f3759df - (i[0] >> 1);  // The magic!
    y[0] = i[0];

    // One iteration of Newton's method for accuracy
    y[0] = y[0] * (1.5 - 0.5 * x * y[0] * y[0]);
    return y[0];
}

// Practical usage: vector normalization
function normalizeVector(x, y, z) {
    const len = Math.sqrt(x*x + y*y + z*z);
    const invLen = fastInvSqrt(x*x + y*y + z*z);
    return { x: x*invLen, y: y*invLen, z: z*invLen };
}

// Comparison: accurate vs fast
console.log(1/Math.sqrt(2));       // 0.70710678...
console.log(fastInvSqrt(2));       // 0.70710678...
Insertion Sort (Nearly-Sorted)
Highly optimized for sorting entities already almost in order, like Z-ordering layers in 2D side-scrollers.
function insertionSort(arr) {
    const n = arr.length;
    for (let i = 1; i < n; i++) {
        const key = arr[i];
        let j = i - 1;

        while (j >= 0 && arr[j] > key) {
            arr[j + 1] = arr[j];
            j--;
        }
        arr[j + 1] = key;
    }
    return arr;
}

// Optimized: binary insertion sort
function binaryInsertionSort(arr) {
    for (let i = 1; i < arr.length; i++) {
        const key = arr[i];
        let lo = 0, hi = i;

        while (lo < hi) {
            const mid = (lo + hi) >> 1;
            if (arr[mid] > key) hi = mid;
            else lo = mid + 1;
        }

        for (let j = i; j > lo; j--) arr[j] = arr[j-1];
        arr[lo] = key;
    }
    return arr;
}

// Adaptive: use insertion sort for small/nearly-sorted arrays
function timSortRun(arr) {
    const RUN = 32;
    for (let i = 0; i < arr.length; i += RUN) {
        const end = Math.min(i + RUN - 1, arr.length - 1);
        insertionSort(arr, i, end);
    }
    return arr;
}
Fisher-Yates Shuffle
Generates an unbiased, randomized permutation. Vital for card games or rolling loot tables fairly.
function fisherYatesShuffle(arr) {
    const shuffled = [...arr];
    for (let i = shuffled.length - 1; i > 0; i--) {
        const j = Math.floor(Math.random() * (i + 1));
        [shuffled[i], shuffled[j]] = [shuffled[j], shuffled[i]];
    }
    return shuffled;
}

// In-place version
function shuffleInPlace(arr) {
    for (let i = arr.length - 1; i > 0; i--) {
        const j = Math.floor(Math.random() * (i + 1));
        [arr[i], arr[j]] = [arr[j], arr[i]];
    }
    return arr;
}

// Seeded PRNG for deterministic shuffle
function seededShuffle(arr, seed) {
    let s = seed;
    const rand = () => {
        s = (s * 16807) % 2147483647;
        return (s - 1) / 2147483646;
    };

    const shuffled = [...arr];
    for (let i = shuffled.length - 1; i > 0; i--) {
        const j = Math.floor(rand() * (i + 1));
        [shuffled[i], shuffled[j]] = [shuffled[j], shuffled[i]];
    }
    return shuffled;
}

// Weighted shuffle for loot tables
function weightedShuffle(items, weights) {
    const result = [...items];
    for (let i = result.length - 1; i > 0; i--) {
        const total = weights.slice(0, i+1).reduce((a,b) => a+b, 0);
        let r = Math.random() * total;
        let j = 0;
        while (r > weights[j]) { r -= weights[j]; j++; }
        [result[i], result[j]] = [result[j], result[i]];
        [weights[i], weights[j]] = [weights[j], weights[i]];
    }
    return result;
}
Linear/Spherical Interpolation (Lerp/Slerp)
Calculates intermediate states between two values or rotational vectors. Essential for smooth animations.
function lerp(a, b, t) {
    return a + (b - a) * t;
}

function lerpVec2(a, b, t) {
    return { x: lerp(a.x, b.x, t), y: lerp(a.y, b.y, t) };
}

// Spherical interpolation (for rotations)
function slerp(v0, v1, t) {
    const dot = v0.x*v1.x + v0.y*v1.y + v0.z*v1.z;
    const theta = Math.acos(Math.min(Math.max(dot, -1), 1));
    const sinTheta = Math.sin(theta);

    if (sinTheta < 0.001) return lerpVec3(v0, v1, t);

    const a = Math.sin((1-t) * theta) / sinTheta;
    const b = Math.sin(t * theta) / sinTheta;
    return {
        x: a*v0.x + b*v1.x,
        y: a*v0.y + b*v1.y,
        z: a*v0.z + b*v1.z
    };
}

// Smoothstep (ease-in-out)
function smoothstep(edge0, edge1, x) {
    const t = Math.max(0, Math.min(1, (x - edge0) / (edge1 - edge0)));
    return t * t * (3 - 2 * t);
}

// Easing functions for animations
const ease = {
    linear: t => t,
    easeIn: t => t*t,
    easeOut: t => t*(2-t),
    easeInOut: t => t<0.5 ? 2*t*t : -1+(4-2*t)*t,
    bounce: t => {
        if (t < 1/2.75) return 7.5625*t*t;
        else if (t < 2/2.75) return 7.5625*(t-=1.5/2.75)*t+.75;
        else if (t < 2.5/2.75) return 7.5625*(t-=2.25/2.75)*t+.9375;
        return 7.5625*(t-=2.625/2.75)*t+.984375;
    }
};
Client-Side Prediction
Simulates a player's inputs locally to mask internet lag, correcting only when server disagrees.
class NetworkPrediction {
    constructor() {
        this.inputBuffer = [];
        this.serverState = { x: 0, y: 0 };
        this.predictedState = { x: 0, y: 0 };
        this.lastAckedInput = 0;
        this.tickRate = 20;  // inputs per second
    }

    applyInput(input) {
        // Store input for reconciliation
        this.inputBuffer.push({ id: this.lastAckedInput++, input });

        // Predict locally immediately
        this.predictedState.x += input.dx;
        this.predictedState.y += input.dy;

        // Send to server (with timestamp)
        return { ...input, timestamp: Date.now() };
    }

    onServerUpdate(serverState, lastProcessedInput) {
        // Discard acknowledged inputs
        this.serverState = serverState;
        this.inputBuffer = this.inputBuffer.filter(
            inp => inp.id > lastProcessedInput
        );

        // Replay unacknowledged inputs on server state
        let reconciled = { ...serverState };
        for (const { input } of this.inputBuffer) {
            reconciled.x += input.dx;
            reconciled.y += input.dy;
        }

        // If difference is small, snap; otherwise interpolate
        const dx = reconciled.x - this.predictedState.x;
        const dy = reconciled.y - this.predictedState.y;
        if (Math.hypot(dx, dy) > 3) {
            this.predictedState = reconciled; // Server correction
        }
    }
}
Mersenne Twister (MT19937)
A PRNG providing fast, high-quality randomness needed for RNG mechanics without predictable loops.
class MersenneTwister {
    constructor(seed = Date.now()) {
        this.N = 624;
        this.M = 397;
        this.mt = new Array(this.N);
        this.mti = this.N + 1;
        this.initGenRand(seed);
    }

    initGenRand(seed) {
        this.mt[0] = seed >>> 0;
        for (this.mti = 1; this.mti < this.N; this.mti++) {
            const s = this.mt[this.mti-1] ^ (this.mt[this.mti-1] >>> 30);
            this.mt[this.mti] = (((s & 0xffff0000) >>> 16) * 1812433253)
                + this.mti + (((s & 0x0000ffff) >>> 0) * 1812433253);
            this.mt[this.mti] >>>= 0;
        }
    }

    genRandInt32() {
        const mag01 = [0x0, 0x9908b0df];
        let y;
        if (this.mti >= this.N) {
            for (let kk = 0; kk < this.N - this.M; kk++) {
                y = (this.mt[kk] & 0x80000000) | (this.mt[kk+1] & 0x7fffffff);
                this.mt[kk] = this.mt[kk+this.M] ^ (y >>> 1) ^ mag01[y & 0x1];
            }
            for (; kk < this.N - 1; kk++) {
                y = (this.mt[kk] & 0x80000000) | (this.mt[kk+1] & 0x7fffffff);
                this.mt[kk] = this.mt[kk+(this.M-this.N)] ^ (y >>> 1) ^ mag01[y & 0x1];
            }
            y = (this.mt[this.N-1] & 0x80000000) | (this.mt[0] & 0x7fffffff);
            this.mt[this.N-1] = this.mt[this.M-1] ^ (y >>> 1) ^ mag01[y & 0x1];
            this.mti = 0;
        }
        y = this.mt[this.mti++];
        y ^= y >>> 11;
        y ^= (y << 7) & 0x9d2c5680;
        y ^= (y << 15) & 0xefc60000;
        y ^= y >>> 18;
        return y >>> 0;
    }

    random() { return this.genRandInt32() * (1/4294967296); }
    intRange(min, max) { return min + Math.floor(this.random() * (max - min + 1)); }
    pick(arr) { return arr[Math.floor(this.random() * arr.length)]; }
}
Douglas-Peucker Algorithm
Simplifies a polyline by reducing vertex points within a threshold. Used to optimize collision boundaries or path networks.
function douglasPeucker(points, epsilon) {
    if (points.length <= 2) return points;

    let maxDist = 0, maxIndex = 0;
    const start = points[0], end = points[points.length-1];

    for (let i = 1; i < points.length - 1; i++) {
        const dist = perpendicularDist(points[i], start, end);
        if (dist > maxDist) {
            maxDist = dist;
            maxIndex = i;
        }
    }

    if (maxDist > epsilon) {
        const left = douglasPeucker(points.slice(0, maxIndex+1), epsilon);
        const right = douglasPeucker(points.slice(maxIndex), epsilon);
        return [...left.slice(0,-1), ...right];
    }

    return [start, end];
}

function perpendicularDist(point, lineStart, lineEnd) {
    const dx = lineEnd.x - lineStart.x;
    const dy = lineEnd.y - lineStart.y;
    const len = Math.sqrt(dx*dx + dy*dy);
    if (len === 0) return Math.hypot(point.x-lineStart.x, point.y-lineStart.y);
    return Math.abs(
        dy*point.x - dx*point.y + lineEnd.x*lineStart.y - lineEnd.y*lineStart.x
    ) / len;
}

//ramerDouglasPeucker: reduces for both X and Y arrays
function simplifyPath(xArr, yArr, epsilon) {
    const points = xArr.map((x, i) => ({x, y: yArr[i]}));
    const simplified = douglasPeucker(points, epsilon);
    return {
        x: simplified.map(p => p.x),
        y: simplified.map(p => p.y)
    };
}
🧬 ADVANCED AI & GROUP DYNAMICS
Complex group tactics, tactical awareness, and modern decision-making algorithms
Goal-Oriented Action Planning (GOAP)
A dynamic AI system where agents evaluate their state, set goals, and dynamically string together actions to create a plan (used in F.E.A.R.).
class GOAP {
    constructor(actions, worldState) {
        this.actions = actions;
        this.worldState = worldState;
    }

    findPlan(goal) {
        const openSet = [{ state: {...this.worldState}, plan: [], cost: 0 }];
        const closedSet = new Set();

        while (openSet.length > 0) {
            openSet.sort((a, b) => a.cost - b.cost);
            const current = openSet.shift();

            if (this.satisfies(current.state, goal)) {
                return current.plan;
            }

            closedSet.add(this.stateHash(current.state));

            for (const action of this.actions) {
                if (!this.canApply(current.state, action)) continue;

                const newState = this.apply(current.state, action);
                const hash = this.stateHash(newState);

                if (closedSet.has(hash)) continue;

                const newPlan = [...current.plan, action.name];
                const newCost = current.cost + action.cost;

                openSet.push({ state: newState, plan: newPlan, cost: newCost });
            }
        }
        return null; // No plan found
    }

    satisfies(state, goal) {
        return Object.entries(goal).every(([k, v]) => state[k] === v);
    }

    canApply(state, action) {
        return Object.entries(action.preconditions).every(([k, v]) => state[k] === v);
    }

    apply(state, action) {
        return { ...state, ...action.effects };
    }

    stateHash(state) {
        return Object.entries(state).sort().join(',');
    }
}

// F.E.A.R.-style combat AI
const worldState = { hasWeapon: true, enemyVisible: false, lowHealth: false };
const actions = [
    { name: 'FindEnemy', preconditions: {}, effects: { enemyVisible: true }, cost: 1 },
    { name: 'TakeCover', preconditions: { enemyVisible: true }, effects: { inCover: true }, cost: 2 },
    { name: 'Attack', preconditions: { enemyVisible: true, hasWeapon: true }, effects: { enemyDead: true }, cost: 3 },
    { name: 'Heal', preconditions: { lowHealth: true }, effects: { lowHealth: false }, cost: 2 }
];
Hierarchical Task Networks (HTN)
Breaks down complex tasks into sub-tasks until reaching primitive, executable actions.
class HTNPlanner {
    constructor(methods, operators) {
        this.methods = methods;
        this.operators = operators;
    }

    plan(worldState, task) {
        if (this.operators[task]) {
            const op = this.operators[task];
            if (this.checkPreconditions(worldState, op.preconditions)) {
                return [{ action: task, params: op }];
            }
            return null;
        }

        const taskMethods = this.methods[task] || [];
        for (const method of taskMethods) {
            if (this.checkPreconditions(worldState, method.preconditions)) {
                let state = { ...worldState };
                const plan = [];

                for (const subtask of method.subtasks) {
                    const subplan = this.plan(state, subtask);
                    if (!subplan) break;
                    plan.push(...subplan);
                    state = this.applyEffects(state, subplan);
                }

                if (plan.length === method.subtasks.length) {
                    return plan;
                }
            }
        }
        return null;
    }

    checkPreconditions(state, preconditions) {
        return Object.entries(preconditions).every(([k, v]) => state[k] === v);
    }

    applyEffects(state, plan) {
        const newState = { ...state };
        for (const step of plan) {
            if (step.params?.effects) {
                Object.assign(newState, step.params.effects);
            }
        }
        return newState;
    }
}

const htnMethods = {

        { preconditions: {}, subtasks: ['ScoutArea', 'EliminateThreats', 'SetPerimeter'] }
    ],
    'EngageEnemy': [
        { preconditions: { hasCover: true }, subtasks: ['TakePosition', 'SuppressEnemy', 'Flank'] }
    ]
};
